class="authorLinkContainer__f13xan AuthorLink__yuu1DZ"><a class="authorNameLink__RYkc-M prc-Link-Link-9ZwDx" data-component="Link" href="/LeanPool/lean-pool/commits?author=claude" aria-label="commits rounded-2 color-border-default mt-2 d-flex flex-column pt-0"><div class="commitMessageContainer__HCH_5X"><span class="ws-pre-wrap f5 wb-break-word text-mono prc-Text-Text-9mHv3" data-component="Text"><div>fix(docs): reuse documentation checkpoints across lakefile edits (<a class="issue-link js-issue-link" data-error-text="Failed to load title" data-id="6247788438" data-permission-text="Title is private" data-url="https://github.com/LeanPool/lean-pool/issues/767" data-hovercard-type="pull_request" data-hovercard-url="/LeanPool/lean-pool/pull/767/hovercard" href="https://github.com/LeanPool/lean-pool/pull/767">#767</a>)</div></span><span class="ws-pre-wrap extended-commit-description-container f6 wb-break-word text-mono mt-2 prc-Text-Text-9mHv3" data-component="Text">The DocsState key hashed lakefile.toml together with the toolchain and
manifest pins, and its only restore key kept that hash. Every lakefile edit
(11 since September) therefore discarded all saved docInfo, forcing a cold
analysis of 31k modules that takes more than one daily run.

Split lakefile.toml into its own key segment and add a fallback restore key
without it. Lake still rebuilds any docInfo whose trace changed, and the
analysis cursor identity still covers lakefile.toml, so a fallback restore
restarts the cursor while reusing every trace that stayed valid.

Co-authored-by: Claude Opus 5.5 &lt;noreply@anthropic.com&gt;</span></div><div class="commitBranchContainer__wieILg" data-files-changed-active="true"><div class="d-flex flex-row
